### **Connor Barwin: Values, Philanthropy, and Investment Approach**

#### **Community Values and Vision**

Connor Barwin’s life and career are rooted in a deep sense of community and civic responsibility. Growing up near Detroit in a civically minded family, he was taught “the importance of taking care and being involved” from an early age[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=Growing%20up%2C%20Connor%20Barwin%E2%80%99s%20parents,give%20back%20to%20the%20neighborhood). Barwin carried those values into his NFL career and beyond. When he arrived in Philadelphia in 2013 to play for the Eagles, he immediately felt a connection with the city’s *“vibrancy, rich history, and unmistakable grit”*[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=When%20Connor%20signed%20a%20six,rich%20history%2C%20and%20unmistakable%20grit). He knew it was **time to take a leadership role in [his] new community** and do everything he could to make it better[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=When%20Barwin%20moved%20to%20Philadelphia,a%20better%20place%2C%E2%80%9D%20he%20says). This drive to **“make the world better”** has been Barwin’s guiding vision. In Philadelphia, he found that what he loves most *“are the people… great people that care deeply for their city”* and whose *“work ethic and grit”* mirror his own values[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=You%20quickly%20became%20something%20of,to%20the%20city%20the%20most). These core beliefs – community engagement, urban renewal, and empowering others – form the foundation of Barwin’s philanthropic and investment philosophy.

Barwin’s leadership style is notably collaborative and inclusive. He has been praised for exhibiting “*‘horizontal’ leadership – a style of civic leadership that leads through collaboration and community empowerment, that convenes stakeholders, listens to them, and helps put them in positions to succeed*”[thephiladelphiacitizen.org](https://thephiladelphiacitizen.org/connor-barwin-retirement/#:~:text=,put%20them%20in%20positions%20to). Rather than imposing his own agenda, Barwin emphasizes listening to the community and working *“with and for the community”* in every initiative[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/about#:~:text=We%20design%20with%20and%20for,the%20community)[thephiladelphiacitizen.org](https://thephiladelphiacitizen.org/connor-barwin-retirement/#:~:text=match%20at%20L244%20MTWB%20doesn%E2%80%99t,lessening%20the%20tensions%20of%20gentrification). This approach stems from lessons he learned in sports: he often says he’s applying what a great coach does – bringing people together as a team, fostering trust, and guiding them toward a common goal[thephiladelphiacitizen.org](https://thephiladelphiacitizen.org/connor-barwin-retirement/#:~:text=match%20at%20L237%20it%E2%80%99s%20because,need%3A%20Philly%20needs%20a%20coach). In short, **Connor Barwin’s values** center on community-driven impact, teamwork, and *“making a difference – big or small – in the lives of others”*[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=%E2%80%9CWhat%20we%20really%20hope%20to,%E2%80%9D). These values influence both his charitable endeavors and how he thinks about investing for the future of his foundation.

#### **Philanthropy Style: The Make The World Better Foundation**

Barwin’s commitment to community took shape in 2013 when he and his family founded the **Make The World Better (MTWB) Foundation** during his first season with the Eagles[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/about#:~:text=Connor%20Barwin%20and%20his%20mom,playing%20for%20the%20Philadelphia%20Eagles). The mission of MTWB is to revitalize public spaces and strengthen communities by creating **inclusive, safe, and inspiring places** for people to gather, play, and thrive[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=vibrancy%2C%20rich%20history%2C%20and%20unmistakable,grit). True to Barwin’s values, the foundation *“works alongside local neighborhoods, ensuring that each project reflects the needs and dreams of the community it serves”*[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=That%20same%20year%2C%20Connor%20and,of%20the%20community%20it%20serves). In practice, this means MTWB **engages residents at every phase** – from site selection and design through construction and upkeep – so that the community has ownership of the project[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/about#:~:text=We%20design%20with%20and%20for,the%20community)[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=While%20the%20construction%20of%20accessible,construction%2C%20opening%2C%20programming%20%26%20maintenance). As Barwin has noted, *“MTWB doesn’t impose – it listens.”* The projects are built *with* the community, not just *for* it, which has even helped ease tensions around gentrification by making improvements a true community-led effort[thephiladelphiacitizen.org](https://thephiladelphiacitizen.org/connor-barwin-retirement/#:~:text=MTWB%20doesn%E2%80%99t%20impose%E2%80%94it%20listens,lessening%20the%20tensions%20of%20gentrification).

One hallmark of Barwin’s philanthropy style is his creative approach to fundraising and partnerships. He has famously merged his love of **sports and music** to rally support for MTWB. Starting in 2014, Barwin began hosting annual **benefit concerts** featuring popular artists – a natural extension for a self-described avid music fan[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=You%20are%20also%20very%20involved,Union%20Transfer%20was%20closed%20that). The first show at Union Transfer starred his friend Kurt Vile and *“raised $170,000, half from the show and half out of Barwin’s pocket.”*[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/future-islands-connor-barwin-make-the-world-better-foundation-20190516.html#:~:text=The%20first%20MTWB%20benefit%20took,half%20out%20of%20Barwin%E2%80%99s%20pocket) Barwin personally matched funds to amplify the impact. These concerts became a Philadelphia summer tradition, growing larger each year and moving to the Dell Music Center by 2017 to accommodate bigger acts and crowds[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/future-islands-connor-barwin-make-the-world-better-foundation-20190516.html#:~:text=Barwin%E2%80%99s%20pocket). After a brief pause in 2020-21, the benefit returned in 2022 and set a record, raising $250,000 in one night (headlined by Japanese Breakfast and others) to help rebuild the Vare Recreation Center[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/connor-barwin-eagles-benefit-concert-alex-g-alvvays-philadelphia-20230228.html#:~:text=Last%20year%E2%80%99s%20show%2C%20which%20marked,Recreation%20Center%20in%20Grays%20Ferry). By 2023, Barwin’s concert featured indie stars Alex G and Alvvays, with *“all proceeds…going to [his] foundation, which rebuilds parks and rec centers in Philadelphia.”*[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/connor-barwin-eagles-benefit-concert-alex-g-alvvays-philadelphia-20230228.html#:~:text=The%20concert%20will%20feature%20two,and%20rec%20centers%20in%20Philadelphia)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/connor-barwin-eagles-benefit-concert-alex-g-alvvays-philadelphia-20230228.html#:~:text=Bailey%2C%20in%202013%2C%20will%20be,venue%2C%20for%20the%20fourth%20time) This innovative fundraising model – leveraging arts and entertainment – showcases Barwin’s inclusive, fun-loving philanthropy style. *“Two of my favorite things in this world are music and sports, and my foundation brings them together to make Philadelphia a better place,”* he says[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=one%20more%20than%20Sean%20Agnew,make%20Philadelphia%20a%20better%20place).

Barwin also isn’t shy about tapping his personal network and resources to advance MTWB’s mission. Philadelphia’s civic and corporate partners have been vital allies. He has brought together “the resources of city government, institutions, professional sports franchises, foundations, local businesses, community members and more” to get projects done[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/about#:~:text=Our%20Partners). For example, MTWB’s early park projects were done in partnership with Urban Roots (a local nonprofit led by Jeffrey Tubbs, who later became MTWB’s co-founder)[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/about#:~:text=MTWB%E2%80%99s%20first%20two%20park%20revitalization,Founder%20at%20MTWB). The foundation works closely with Philadelphia’s Parks & Recreation department and the city’s Rebuild initiative on major renovations. Barwin even engaged fellow athletes and coaches: he frequently invited Eagles teammates to attend MTWB events and groundbreakings[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=The%20sold,donated%20by%20James%20Beard%20Award), and former Mayor Michael Nutter became a big supporter, praising Barwin’s bias for action (***“Most folks talk about what they want to do. Connor Barwin talks about what he’s going to do.”***[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/news/barwin-unveils-revitalized-park-15793032#:~:text=Alex%20Smith)). In addition, Barwin has served as a spokesperson for causes aligned with his values – like promoting public transit and sustainable energy. During his Eagles days he was an official **SEPTA rider-spokesman** and drove an electric car around Philly[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=with%20the%20Eagles,maternity%20center%20and%20a%20school), and he partnered with sponsor NRG Energy on trips to Haiti to install solar panels at community centers[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=with%20the%20Eagles,maternity%20center%20and%20a%20school). All of these efforts underscore how Barwin’s philanthropic style is hands-on and holistic: he raises capital creatively, contributes his own money and platform, and builds coalitions to **“make their neighborhood, and the world, a better place to live”**[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=,to%20grow%20and%20to%20learn)[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=show%20for%20fans%2C%20but%20all,to%20grow%20and%20to%20learn).

**Impact:** Over the past decade, Barwin and MTWB have invested more than **$30 million** into Philadelphia neighborhood redevelopment[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=Over%20the%20past%20decade%2C%20MTWB,authentic%20partnerships%2C%20and%20meaningful%20outcomes)[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/team/front-office/connor-barwin#:~:text=focus%20on%20youth%20engagement%2C%20stewardship,Brooks%20Park%20in%20Point%20Breeze). The foundation’s projects range from playgrounds and athletic fields to community centers that host after-school programs and arts events[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=Over%20the%20past%20decade%2C%20MTWB,authentic%20partnerships%2C%20and%20meaningful%20outcomes). Major accomplishments include:

- **Ralph Brooks Park (Point Breeze)** – A once-neglected inner-city park transformed with a new basketball court, playground (“tot lot”), community garden, and mural in 2015[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=The%20MTWB%20Foundation%2C%20founded%20by,org)[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/news/barwin-unveils-revitalized-park-15793032#:~:text=On%20Monday%2C%20Barwin%20saw%20one,Violators%2C%20The%20Districts%20and%20others). This $450,000 rejuvenation was MTWB’s first project, funded by Barwin’s inaugural 2014 concert[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/news/barwin-unveils-revitalized-park-15793032#:~:text=On%20Monday%2C%20Barwin%20saw%20one,Violators%2C%20The%20Districts%20and%20others). *Outcome:* *“Since the park reopened in 2015, the community gardens have flourished, a basketball league has formed and grown, and the court is in constant use.”*[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=would%20you%20say%20is%20the,turf%20football%20and%20baseball%20fields) It’s a model success story of a public space coming back to life.
- **Smith Playground (South Philly)** – A sprawling 7.5-acre park rehab, including a renovated rec center building and brand-new turf football and baseball fields[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=Courtesy%20of%20Barwin%20and%20his,kick%20off%20in%20Summer%202016)[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=thrived%20since%20the%20park%20reopened,turf%20football%20and%20baseball%20fields). Barwin’s 2015 concert raised $300,000 toward Smith Playground’s $2.5M overhaul[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=PHILADELPHIA%20%28CBS%29%20,held%20on%20Saturday%2C%20June%2020)[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=a%20benefit%20concert%20at%20Union,held%20on%20Saturday%2C%20June%2020). By 2018, Smith’s final phase was completed, making it MTWB’s largest project to date[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=thrived%20since%20the%20park%20reopened,turf%20football%20and%20baseball%20fields).
- **Waterloo Playground (Kensington)** – A playground and public space renovation in an underserved neighborhood of North Philly. This project, completed with community input, added safe play equipment and amenities. *(Barwin’s foundation also facilitated youth yoga and wellness programs here*[*mtwb.org*](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=Image%3A%20Vare%20gymnastics%20team%20during,Vare%20groundbreaking%20ceremony)*.)*
- **Vare Recreation Center (Grays Ferry)** – The most ambitious project, a full rebuild of a historic rec center and grounds. Done in partnership with the city’s Rebuild program, Vare’s revitalization carried a budget over $20 million and involved extensive community engagement[instagram.com](https://www.instagram.com/p/C6_hZHUOsyj/#:~:text=At%20MTWB%2C%20community%20engagement%20and,the%20support%20of%20former). Barwin kicked off fundraising for Vare with his 2019 and 2022 benefit shows[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/connor-barwin-eagles-benefit-concert-alex-g-alvvays-philadelphia-20230228.html#:~:text=Last%20year%E2%80%99s%20show%2C%20which%20marked,Recreation%20Center%20in%20Grays%20Ferry) and worked alongside city officials and local youth (like the Vare gymnastics team) to design a state-of-the-art facility[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/underdogs#:~:text=After%20their%20beloved%20recreation%20center,on%20their%20journey%20back%20home). The project had *“many twists and turns”* but by 2023 the finish line was in sight[facebook.com](https://www.facebook.com/mtwbFoundation/#:~:text=Facebook%20www,student%20in%20our%20Youth%20Project)[instagram.com](https://www.instagram.com/p/C6_hZHUOsyj/#:~:text=At%20MTWB%2C%20community%20engagement%20and,the%20support%20of%20former). Vare’s reopening will leave a lasting legacy, giving South Philadelphia families a modern hub for sports, arts and community support.
Beyond the construction of parks, MTWB emphasizes **ongoing community stewardship** of these spaces. Barwin’s team runs programs to cultivate local “playground stewards” and youth leaders who will care for the facilities[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=While%20the%20construction%20of%20accessible,construction%2C%20opening%2C%20programming%20%26%20maintenance)[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=MTWB%20works%20with%20numerous%20partners,wellness%20of%20the%20surrounding%20community). They host community events – from design workshops and volunteer build days to holiday turkey giveaways and ribbon-cuttings – to ensure neighbors feel connected and invested in their parks[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=work%2C%20these%20projects%20serve%20as,construction%2C%20opening%2C%20programming%20%26%20maintenance)[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=MTWB%20orchestrates%20a%20variety%20of,communities%20we%20serve%20each%20year). There’s even a Youth Project Team in partnership with a mentor program, engaging Philadelphia high school students in the design and construction process (and awarding scholarships to those who participate)[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=MTWB%E2%80%99s%20Youth%20Project%20Team%20is,program%20are%20eligible%20for%20scholarships)[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=project%20and%20is%20done%20in,program%20are%20eligible%20for%20scholarships). All these efforts reinforce Barwin’s philanthropic approach of empowerment and sustainability. As he learned during his time at Wharton, it’s critical to think about *“the sustainability of a nonprofit, and the importance of building strong partnerships to keep the work going.”*[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=,%E2%80%9D) In practice, Barwin has built MTWB not just to complete one-off projects, but to **inspire a culture of ongoing community improvement** – true to the foundation’s name, making the world better one neighborhood at a time.

#### **Investment Background and Business Ventures**

While Connor Barwin is best known for his exploits on the football field and in the community, he has also developed a notable background in business and investment. After a 10-year NFL career (2009–2018) in which he achieved All-Pro honors and became a fan-favorite in Philadelphia[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/team/front-office/connor-barwin#:~:text=After%20spending%20his%20first%20four,5%20sacks), Barwin began preparing for “life after football” even before he hung up his cleats. He had always been intrigued by organizational leadership and finance – interests that led him to pursue an **MBA from the Wharton School**. In 2021, a year after formally retiring as a player, Barwin enrolled in Wharton’s executive MBA program, and he graduated in 2023[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/team/front-office/connor-barwin#:~:text=Conference%20selection%20at%20the%20University,of%20the%20University%20of%20Pennsylvania). He describes it as *“one of the hardest things I’ve ever done… but also one of the most rewarding”*, noting that returning to a competitive classroom environment and learning new “hard skills” was invaluable[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=%E2%80%9CIt%20was%20probably%20one%20of,hope%20I%20helped%20a%20couple)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=classroom%20and%20that%20same%20sort,hope%20I%20helped%20a%20couple). His Wharton coursework in areas like management communication and data analytics had immediate practical benefits – he credits one class with giving him the tools to implement a new program for Eagles players *“that no other team in the league is doing,”* persuading others with data and storytelling[penntoday.upenn.edu](https://penntoday.upenn.edu/news/connor-barwin-nfl-wharton-and-making-world-better-place#:~:text=%E2%80%9CBeing%20surrounded%20by%20my%20classmates,storytelling%20to%20effectively%20communicate%20ideas)[penntoday.upenn.edu](https://penntoday.upenn.edu/news/connor-barwin-nfl-wharton-and-making-world-better-place#:~:text=%E2%80%9CFrom%20this%20one%20class%2C%20I,%E2%80%9D). Overall, Barwin says Wharton “*taught me how to ask better questions, and gave me the confidence to know I can find the answer*.”[penntoday.upenn.edu](https://penntoday.upenn.edu/news/connor-barwin-nfl-wharton-and-making-world-better-place#:~:text=%E2%80%9CBeing%20surrounded%20by%20my%20classmates,storytelling%20to%20effectively%20communicate%20ideas)[penntoday.upenn.edu](https://penntoday.upenn.edu/news/connor-barwin-nfl-wharton-and-making-world-better-place#:~:text=%E2%80%9CFrom%20this%20one%20class%2C%20I,%E2%80%9D) This analytical and strategic mindset is now part of his approach to both football operations and nonprofit leadership[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=After%20retiring%20from%20the%20NFL,ensure%20the%20foundation%E2%80%99s%20impact%20endures).

Concurrently with earning his MBA, Barwin transitioned into a **front-office role with the Philadelphia Eagles**. He started as a special assistant to the GM in 2020, then became Director of Player Development in 2022, and by 2024 he was promoted to **Head of Football Development and Strategy** for the team[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/team/front-office/connor-barwin#:~:text=Connor%20Barwin%20enters%20his%20sixth,of%20football%20development%20and%20strategy)[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/team/front-office/connor-barwin#:~:text=Prior%20to%20being%20named%20to,21). In these roles, Barwin straddles the worlds of sports and business, applying his strategic thinking to player mentoring, roster construction, and long-term team planning. This experience indicates his comfort managing complex projects and working with data-driven decision-making – skills that translate well into investment management. Indeed, Barwin has shown a keen interest in **sports entrepreneurship and global investment opportunities**. While at Wharton, he and a group of classmates formed an investment consortium called *Cotogna Sports Group* that made headlines in early 2023: they pooled $5 million to purchase a **90% stake in the Italian professional basketball team Pallacanestro Trieste**[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=Barwin%20and%20five%20Penn%20classmates,old%20club%20into%20a%20European). For Barwin, a lifelong basketball fan who even played college hoops at Cincinnati, becoming a co-owner of a European basketball club was a dream opportunity. But it was also a business venture he approached seriously – the Wharton group aimed to turn the 50-year-old Trieste club into a competitive, modern franchise.

Barwin’s involvement in the **Trieste investment** reveals much about his risk appetite and strategic outlook. The team was fighting to avoid relegation when they bought it, and Barwin joked that watching those high-stakes games *“probably lost [him] a couple days of [his] life”* due to stress[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=Connor%20Barwin%20was%20in%20his,point%20victory). Yet, he and his partners were undeterred by short-term setbacks: *“It won’t be easy … but [Cotogna Sports Group] is in it for the long haul,”* one partner said, emphasizing their long-term commitment despite the club dropping to the second division[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=Barwin%20and%20five%20Penn%20classmates,club%20into%20a%20European%20power)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=%C2%BB%20READ%20MORE%3A%20Joel%20Embiid,not%20trying%20to%20be%20one). Barwin echoed this patience, focusing on building infrastructure and using analytics to improve the team over time[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=%E2%80%9COnce%20we%20hire%20the%20GM%2C,%E2%80%9D)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=They%E2%80%99ll%20instead%20focus%20on%20analytics%2C,control%20can%20provide%20the%20difference). In fact, Barwin took charge of recruiting a new general manager for Trieste and bringing “Moneyball”-style data analysis to a sports culture that hadn’t fully embraced it[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=January%20to%20purchase%2090,club%20into%20a%20European%20power)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=The%20initial%20group%20%E2%80%94%20Reid%2C,He%20was%20in). This demonstrates Barwin’s inclination to invest not just capital but also expertise into ventures he believes in – a hands-on, value-add investor. His group is already eyeing additional sports team acquisitions (including lower-tier soccer clubs) as future deals[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=relegation%20by%20not%20finishing%20the,the%20league%E2%80%99s%20three%20worst%20teams). This foray into international sports ownership indicates **Barwin is willing to take calculated risks for higher returns and impactful outcomes**. It’s an unconventional investment, but one aligned with his passions (sports, community fervor) and leveraging his knowledge of team-building and fan engagement[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=But%20he%20wasn%E2%80%99t%20fully%20aware,%E2%80%9D)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=Nord%20%E2%80%94%20leading%20the%20way).

Outside of sports, Barwin hasn’t publicized many personal investments, but he has consistently aligned his financial decisions with his values. For example, he literally “invested” in Philadelphia’s future by funding park projects and matching donations through MTWB[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/future-islands-connor-barwin-make-the-world-better-foundation-20190516.html#:~:text=The%20first%20MTWB%20benefit%20took,half%20out%20of%20Barwin%E2%80%99s%20pocket)[cbsnews.com](https://www.cbsnews.com/philadelphia/news/connor-barwin-mtwb-foundation-raises-300000/#:~:text=a%20benefit%20concert%20at%20Union,held%20on%20Saturday%2C%20June%2020). He also invested in **sustainable energy for his own home**, partnering with a local solar company to install solar panels – supporting a local business and clean energy cause at once[solar-states.com](https://www.solar-states.com/mtwb/#:~:text=Connor%20Barwin%20Goes%20Solar%20,). As an investor, Barwin seems to gravitate towards opportunities where he can blend purpose with profit. His **education in history (B.A. from University of Cincinnati) and business (MBA from Wharton)**[**csg.ventures**](https://www.csg.ventures/connor-barwin#:~:text=Connor%20Barwin%20,MBA%20from%20the%20Wharton), combined with a decade of leadership in professional sports, have given him a broad perspective. Those who know him often highlight his unique ability to connect the dots between sectors – he writes a column on civic issues[thephiladelphiacitizen.org](https://thephiladelphiacitizen.org/about/the-philadelphia-citizen-masthead/connor-barwin/#:~:text=Connor%20Barwin%20,Civic%20Season%2C%20in%20which), speaks out on urban policy, and clearly understands that business decisions can drive social outcomes. This bodes well for his capacity to oversee an investment portfolio that must achieve financial growth *and* fund positive community impact.

#### **Risk Tolerance and Investment Goals for MTWB**

Connor Barwin’s **risk tolerance** can be inferred from both his stated financial goals for MTWB and his personal ventures. In managing the foundation’s funds, Barwin balances an ambitious vision with a pragmatic appreciation for sustainability. He has a **clear goal** for the MTWB endowment: grow an initial $500,000 investment into **at least $1.5 million over the next 10 years**, while making modest annual withdrawals to support ongoing community programs[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=Connor%20would%20like%20to%20grow,lasting%20community%20pride). Specifically, starting at the end of year 3 (2029), Barwin plans to **draw $10,000 per year** from the fund to channel directly into grassroots projects, with the hope of increasing those annual community contributions over time as the fund grows[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=revitalize%20public%20spaces%20and%20inspire,lasting%20community%20pride). By 2036 (a decade out), the target is to have a robust $1.5M+ fund ready for a *“truly transformative capital project”* in Philadelphia[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=Connor%20would%20like%20to%20grow,lasting%20community%20pride)[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=By%202036%2C%20MTWB%E2%80%99s%20fund%20will,visible%20legacy%20for%20Philadelphia%E2%80%99s%20neighborhoods). This financial plan tells us a few key things about Barwin’s risk profile:

- **Growth-Oriented:** Tripling the fund in 10 years (even while taking distributions) will require significant growth, likely outpacing inflation by a wide margin. This implies Barwin is willing to accept a **moderate-to-high growth investment strategy** – one that probably involves equities or higher-return assets – rather than keeping the foundation money in ultra-conservative instruments. In other words, he knows the fund must work hard to meet the $1.5M goal, indicating comfort with some level of market risk to achieve long-term returns.
- **Long-Term Horizon:** Barwin’s timeline is a full decade, which aligns with his general long-horizon mindset (seen also in his sports investments). He is not looking for quick wins or short-term speculation. Instead, he emphasizes that MTWB’s impact and the big project are planned for *“10 years over”*, requiring patience and steady growth[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=Connor%20would%20like%20to%20grow,lasting%20community%20pride)[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=By%202036%2C%20MTWB%E2%80%99s%20fund%20will,visible%20legacy%20for%20Philadelphia%E2%80%99s%20neighborhoods). This long view allows for a higher risk tolerance in the interim, because short-term market fluctuations can be ridden out in favor of the long-run trend. His own words reinforce this approach: Wharton taught him to step back and use a strategic lens, prioritizing **enduring impact over immediate results**[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=After%20retiring%20from%20the%20NFL,ensure%20the%20foundation%E2%80%99s%20impact%20endures). We see a parallel in how he approached owning Trieste – despite an immediate setback (relegation), he remained focused on the **“long haul”** improvements[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=It%20won%E2%80%99t%20be%20easy%20%E2%80%94,Barwin%20in%20his%20Philadelphia%20basement)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=relegation%20by%20not%20finishing%20the,the%20league%E2%80%99s%20three%20worst%20teams).
- **Controlled Drawdown (Capital Preservation Awareness):** The fact that Barwin limits the annual draw to $10k (just 2% of the initial $500k) in the early years shows a degree of caution and preservation. He’s not trying to withdraw an unsustainable amount; in fact, $10k is relatively conservative, likely less than the fund’s expected yearly earnings if invested well. This suggests he wants the **principal to keep growing** and is mindful of not eroding it. The drawdown could even be viewed as a “floor” – a guaranteed community contribution each year – while still allowing the bulk of returns to compound. As the fund grows, he’d only raise the payout in proportion, indicating a **balanced, endowment-style approach** (spending a small percent and reinvesting the rest for growth)[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=revitalize%20public%20spaces%20and%20inspire,lasting%20community%20pride). Such an approach aligns with moderate risk tolerance: he is not so aggressive as to spend down the fund, but he’s confident enough in growth to start giving back from year 3 onwards.
- **Partnerships and Diversification:** Barwin’s emphasis on building strong partnerships for MTWB[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=,%E2%80%9D) hints that he values spreading risk and not relying on any single source of success. In philanthropy, this means diversifying funding streams (grants, donations, events) and collaborating widely so that the foundation’s work isn’t vulnerable to one point of failure. By analogy, in investing, one would expect Barwin to favor a **well-diversified portfolio** that can weather different market conditions. His own comment that Wharton gave him a “broader, systems-level perspective” likely applies here – he’ll look at the big picture and mitigate risks through strategic asset allocation[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/case-study/#:~:text=After%20retiring%20from%20the%20NFL,ensure%20the%20foundation%E2%80%99s%20impact%20endures). We also see his comfort with **innovative strategies** – for instance, he embraced analytics in sports and new ideas learned in B-school to gain an edge[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=%E2%80%9CIt%E2%80%99s%20easy%20to%20say%2C%20%E2%80%98We%E2%80%99re,%E2%80%9D)[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=They%E2%80%99ll%20instead%20focus%20on%20analytics%2C,control%20can%20provide%20the%20difference). This could translate into openness to modern investment approaches (data-driven analysis, perhaps ESG investing that aligns with MTWB’s ethos, etc.), as long as they fit the long-term goal.
In summary, Connor Barwin can be characterized as having a **moderate risk tolerance with a growth-oriented bent**. He is **not risk-averse** – his career moves and projects show he’s willing to venture into new territory (whether it’s buying a foreign sports team or spearheading multi-million-dollar community builds) with confidence. He even describes the adrenaline of these ventures as part of what drives him, noting *“there’s nothing like it”* when describing the thrill of being invested in a team’s performance[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=%E2%80%9CI%20probably%20lost%20a%20couple,former%20Eagles%20Pro%20Bowl%20linebacker). However, he is also **not reckless** – every risk he takes is tied to a thoughtful strategy and a long-term purpose. For the MTWB fund, Barwin’s top priority is to **secure the foundation’s future impact**. That means he will likely favor an investment strategy that can achieve strong returns (to hit the 10-year target) but also **protect the capital enough to fund the annual community programs and the big 2036 project**. He summed up his philosophy well in an interview: *“I know I have 30 more years of trying to add value, make the world better, and do things to help people”*, so he invested in himself (through Wharton) to gain the skills to do it right[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=classroom%20and%20that%20same%20sort,hope%20I%20helped%20a%20couple). This mindset implies a careful stewardship of resources – taking on appropriate risk to grow them, but always with the mission in mind. As an investor for MTWB, we can expect Barwin to support a portfolio that aligns with his values (possibly favoring socially responsible investments or companies that tie into community well-being) and one that is managed with both **enthusiasm and prudence**. In practice, our team should be prepared to propose a diversified mix – likely equity-heavy for growth, but with risk controls – and to justify how it will meet Connor’s goals while reflecting his community-driven ethos. By knowing our client’s values and background this deeply, we can tailor an investment philosophy that *“aligns with [our] team’s strategy”* and wins his confidence[globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu](https://globalyouth.wharton.upenn.edu/competitions/investment-competition/deliverables/#:~:text=Requirements%20%26%20Deliverables%20,Wharton%20Global%20Youth%20Program).

#### **Current Initiatives and Outlook for MTWB**

As of 2025, Connor Barwin’s foundation and career are at an exciting juncture. MTWB has completed four major site revitalizations in Philadelphia and is poised to take on its next challenge. The **Vare Recreation Center** project – a cornerstone of recent years – is nearing completion and stands as a testament to MTWB’s evolved capacity. This large-scale project, done in concert with city funding and community advocacy, will give South Philadelphia a “new home” for its beloved gymnastics team and many other programs[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/underdogs#:~:text=After%20their%20beloved%20recreation%20center,on%20their%20journey%20back%20home). The story of the Vare rec center even inspired a documentary film (*“The Underdogs”*) that follows the resilient youth and coach who campaigned for their facility to be rebuilt[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/underdogs#:~:text=After%20their%20beloved%20recreation%20center,on%20their%20journey%20back%20home). Such storytelling underscores MTWB’s impact not just in bricks-and-mortar terms, but in human terms – it’s changing lives and inspiring others to get involved.

Looking ahead, MTWB is **committed to ensuring the longevity** of the parks and centers it has revitalized. Barwin often mentions *“stewardship”* and *“programming”* as critical next steps[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=work%2C%20these%20projects%20serve%20as,construction%2C%20opening%2C%20programming%20%26%20maintenance)[mtwb.org](https://www.mtwb.org/community-engagement#:~:text=Image). We can expect the foundation to focus on supporting community advisory councils, organizing youth sports leagues and arts activities in the new spaces, and perhaps training local leaders to take ownership. Barwin’s approach has always been to work **“side-by-side”** with the community[phillymag.com](https://www.phillymag.com/sponsor-content/health-hero-vip-how-connor-barwin-gives-back/#:~:text=The%20Make%20the%20World%20Better,make%20that%20dream%20a%20reality), so as MTWB grows its portfolio of projects, it will likely also grow its network of volunteers, donors, and neighborhood partners in each locale. The foundation is also branching out into events beyond concerts – for example, they’ve hosted fundraising sports tournaments (a citywide **MTWB Pickleball Tournament** was announced for summer 2024)[x.com](https://x.com/mtwbFoundation#:~:text=MTWB%20Foundation%20,28th%2C%20as%20the%20summer%27s), engaging a broader audience in the mission. Barwin’s ability to rally both everyday citizens and prominent figures (from pro athletes like Jason Kelce and Malcolm Jenkins, to corporate sponsors and city officials) means MTWB’s initiatives will continue to blend fun, philanthropy, and civic pride.

In his professional role with the Eagles, Barwin is likewise making a broad impact. As **Head of Football Development and Strategy**, he is helping shape the culture and competitive strategy of a franchise that means a great deal to Philadelphia[philadelphiaeagles.com](https://www.philadelphiaeagles.com/team/front-office/connor-barwin#:~:text=Connor%20Barwin%20enters%20his%20sixth,of%20football%20development%20and%20strategy). He has been described as a bridge between players and management, someone who can mentor young athletes (instilling in them the same spirit of community involvement he embodies) while also contributing to high-level decisions. The fact that he was the **executive producer of the Eagles’ Christmas album** that raised $1.25 million for charity – *“A Philly Special Christmas”* in 2022 – shows how Barwin merges his roles[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/entertainment/music/connor-barwin-eagles-benefit-concert-alex-g-alvvays-philadelphia-20230228.html#:~:text=Barwin%2C%20an%20avid%20music%20fan,25%20million%20for%20Philly%20charities). He leveraged his football connections (teammates singing carols) to create something that benefited the entire city, with funds going to various nonprofits. This crossover initiative won national praise and is a hallmark of Barwin’s innovative, no-boundaries approach to doing good. For our investment team, it’s a reminder that Connor Barwin will appreciate creativity and social consciousness even in how we construct his portfolio. He is likely to value **impact investing** or at least a strategy that does no harm to the causes he cares about, all else being equal.

Finally, with Barwin’s recent completion of his MBA and the sports venture in Italy, he has a **broader lens on the world of investing**. He has effectively become an international investor and a savvy business strategist, while remaining at heart a community activist. The next 10 years for him and MTWB will be about scaling up – scaling the foundation’s fund (hence this investment competition case), scaling their project scope (perhaps tackling more expensive, city-wide initiatives), and maybe even scaling his personal influence (some speculate he could run for public office one day given his civic leadership profile[thephiladelphiacitizen.org](https://thephiladelphiacitizen.org/connor-barwin-retirement/#:~:text=match%20at%20L268%20Barwin%20will,telling%20me%2C%20%E2%80%9CI%20don%E2%80%99t%20have), though he’s made no official step in that direction). What is clear is that Connor Barwin is **dedicated to Philadelphia for the long term**. He’s raising his family in the city[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=Barwin%20grew%20up%20outside%20Detroit%2C,are%20raising%20their%20two%20children), he proudly calls himself a Philadelphian, and he continuously finds ways to give back. As he stated, *“I have 30 more years of trying to add value… and do things to help people”*, and gaining business acumen was part of that plan[inquirer.com](https://www.inquirer.com/sports/connor-barwin-eagles-wharton-pallacanestro-trieste-20230513.html#:~:text=Eagles-,Former%20Eagles%20star%20Connor%20Barwin%20leaves%20Wharton%20with%20an%20MBA,purchase%2090%25%20of%20Pallacanestro%20Trieste.#:~:text=classroom%20and%20that%20same%20sort,hope%20I%20helped%20a%20couple). Our team’s job is to ensure that the financial side of his vision – growing MTWB’s assets – is handled with the same excellence and integrity that Barwin himself brings to everything he does. By understanding his values, philanthropy style, investment background, and current initiatives in depth, we can craft an investment strategy that truly resonates with Connor Barwin and helps **Make The World Better** – both financially and socially – over the decade to come.

### **Wharton Global High School Investment Competition Final Judge Prompt**

#### **I. Context and Purpose**

You are serving as an **official judge** for the **Wharton Global High School Investment Competition**, one of the world’s most recognized experiential finance challenges for secondary students. You are responsible for evaluating the **Final Report** of a student team working on behalf of **Connor Barwin**, founder of the **Make The World Better (MTWB)** Foundation. Your task is to assess whether the team’s strategic reasoning, portfolio construction, and communication quality meet the high expectations of Wharton Global Youth Program standards.

The competition simulates the work of real analysts and investment committees, emphasizing judgment, research discipline, and teamwork. As a judge, you are replicating the perspective of **Wharton faculty, investment professionals, and endowment allocators** who review student submissions in real competitions.

##### **Client & Investment Mandate**

- **Client:** Connor Barwin — Wharton MBA (2023), former NFL executive, and civic innovator known for leading sustainable community transformation projects in Philadelphia.
- **Capital Objective:** Grow **$500,000 → $1.5 million within 10 years**, while enabling **≈$10,000 annual withdrawals starting in year 3** to fund park stewardship, youth education, and local revitalization.
- **Investment Philosophy:** Barwin believes that sustainable investing must balance *measured risk* with *measurable good*. His outlook fuses long-term capital stewardship with transparency, accountability, and regeneration of social value.
- **Educational Intent:** The Wharton competition evaluates how teams think, justify, and communicate — not how much their simulated portfolio earns. Judges value clarity, analytical maturity, and learning progression over short-term performance.

##### **Official Wharton Judging Pillars**

The competition uses four formal assessment categories, expanded here with judging focus areas:

- **Investment Strategy & Process** – Coherence, originality, and intellectual depth behind portfolio design and rebalancing.
- **Client Knowledge & Objectives** – Insightful translation of the client’s goals, values, and constraints into actionable portfolio decisions.
- **Portfolio Analysis** – Evaluation of diversification, risk-return calibration, and quantitative reasoning supporting the investment approach.
- **Creativity & Presentation** – Originality, teamwork, conceptual innovation, and clarity of communication.
By the end of the evaluation and going through all the criterias that will be described as below, give a probability from 0% to 100% of the team's chance of advancing to semifinalist based on its quality and performance in both its midterm report and final report. Keep in mind that the final report is the primary focus with its weighting in semifinalist consideration much more than the midterm report.

#### **II. Judge Mindset and Evaluation Philosophy**

You are judging not as a teacher grading homework, but as a **Wharton faculty panelist or professional portfolio manager** reviewing a live client proposal. You should read as if deciding whether this team could present credibly to an actual investment committee.

- **Depth of Reasoning:** Look beyond slogans or stock commentary — assess whether causal logic connects data to decisions.
- **Feasibility:** Evaluate whether the growth path, drawdown assumptions, and liquidity plan are plausible for MTWB’s needs.
- **Process and Learning:** Reward teamwork evidence, iteration, and the integration of feedback, which reflect authentic professional growth.
- **Professional Discipline:** Clear structure, brevity, and organization demonstrate maturity. The best reports feel like polished Wharton undergraduate investment memos.
- **Integrity and Ethics:** MTWB’s purpose centers on stewardship; authenticity and ethical transparency must underlie financial claims.
Avoid rhetorical excess, jargon, or ESG generalities without proof. Reward intellectual honesty, balanced realism, and stewardship-driven decision logic.

#### **III. Evaluation Procedure**

- Review the **Final Report** in full (e.g., *Wharton Investment Competition ’25 – GREENOX Final Report*).
- Extract its **central thesis**, **portfolio construction logic**, and **decision-making framework**.
- Assess all eight categories listed below, scoring each 1–10. Provide **3–6 sentences** of feedback grounded in textual evidence.
- Quote or paraphrase **brief (<20 words)** sections where useful. Offer **specific, actionable improvements** rather than generic praise or criticism.
- Record your final decision in the weighted scorecard.

#### **IV. Scoring Dimensions (Each /10)**

##### **1. Investment Strategy & Process**

- Is there a unique, data-driven investment philosophy that ties research to structure?
- Does the process show repeatability and self-correction under new information?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Distinct frameworks (e.g., *Greenox Matrix*), thematic consistency, measurable link between philosophy and execution, evidence of process discipline.

##### **2. Client Knowledge & Objectives**

- How clearly does the report align with MTWB’s liquidity timeline and stewardship goals?
- Does it translate Barwin’s values into measurable investment criteria?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Transparent mapping of holdings to mission and liquidity needs; demonstration of Barwin’s emphasis on patience and community benefit.

##### **3. Portfolio Analysis**

- Are asset allocations logically justified and supported by data?
- Are risk management tools, volatility assumptions, and drawdown limits defined?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Clear quantitative backing, defined Preserve–Pursue–Pioneer balance, dynamic rebalancing logic, and explicit downside planning.

##### **4. Analytical & Financial Rigor**

- Are financial metrics, valuation multiples, and macroeconomic variables properly interpreted?
- Are assumptions explained and stress-tested?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Sophisticated yet accurate use of beta, compounding math, and cross-sector analysis; macro awareness integrated with mission fidelity.

##### **5. Communication & Presentation**

- Is the document polished, readable, and logically structured for a judging panel?
- Does the visual presentation enhance comprehension rather than distract?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Executive-style tone, organized layout, concise phrasing, and consistency of visuals, graphs, and section transitions.

##### **6. Creativity & Insight**

- Does the team introduce novel models or frameworks that add explanatory value?
- Are creative ideas grounded in credible analysis rather than aesthetics?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Creative integration of ethical and quantitative insights, visually innovative but analytically strong exhibits, and mission-inspired analogies.

##### **7. Critical Thinking**

- Does the team show awareness of opportunity cost and decision alternatives?
- Are trade-offs and assumptions examined transparently?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Explicit acknowledgment of risk-return tensions; willingness to question own biases; well-reasoned sector substitutions and sensitivity tables.

##### **8. Overall Impact & Competition Readiness**

- Does the report exhibit finalist-level polish, realism, and coherence?
- Would this portfolio inspire confidence from actual Wharton judges and investment professionals?
- **Indicators of Excellence:** Fully aligned mission and metrics, professional maturity, realistic projections, and a distinct narrative identity.

#### **V. Output Format**

1. Investment Strategy & Process – ?/10

Justification: …

2. Client Knowledge & Objectives – ?/10

Justification: …

3. Portfolio Analysis – ?/10

Justification: …

4. Analytical & Financial Rigor – ?/10

Justification: …

5. Communication & Presentation – ?/10

Justification: …

6. Creativity & Insight – ?/10

Justification: …

7. Critical Thinking & Trade-Off Discipline – ?/10

Justification: …

8. Overall Impact & Competition Readiness – ?/10

Justification: …

Overall Summary (300-400 words)

Summarize key strengths, the single largest weakness, the most critical refinement needed, and the final decision.

#### **VI. Judge Calibration and Decision Tools**

##### **Weighted Scoring Anchors**

- Investment Strategy & Process – **25%**
- Client Knowledge & Objectives – **20%**
- Portfolio Analysis – **20%**
- Analytical & Financial Rigor – **10%**
- Communication & Presentation – **5%**
- Creativity & Insight – **10%**
- Critical Thinking & Trade-Off Discipline – **10%**

##### **Behavioral Anchors**

**9–10 (Outstanding):** Conceptually original, numerically feasible, highly professional; client mission integrated throughout.

**7–8 (Competent):** Coherent, well-written, minor clarity issues or underdeveloped quant logic.

**5–6 (Adequate):** Functional structure, uneven depth, or inconsistent application of risk control.

**3–4 (Weak):** Incomplete reasoning, speculative claims, or unclear mission fit.

**1–2 (Deficient):** Rule violations, fabrication, or disconnected logic.

##### **Feasibility and Integrity Checklist**

- Ten-year compounding path to $1.5M must be mathematically supportable.
- $10K annual withdrawals must be funded through income or buffer, not forced asset sales.
- Portfolio volatility must match MTWB’s moderate-risk stance.
- Sector or asset concentration should not exceed 40% per sleeve.
- Stress scenarios must show liquidity coverage under 20% market downturn.
- Ethical compliance: no prohibited or speculative instruments.
- Proper sourcing: all data cited or referenced accurately.

##### **Red-Flag Gates (Automatic Below-Threshold)**

- Plagiarism or unverifiable statistics.
- Ignored liquidity management.
- No articulated risk process.
- Misaligned mission narrative.
- Conflicts between stated thesis and holdings.
Finally, give on page summary scoreboard as described below:

Portfolio Analysis

##### **One-Page Summary Scorecard**

Produce the following in tables: For each give notes and weighted score.

**Category 1: Investment Strategy & Process (25%)** — Assess the distinctiveness and coherence of the investment philosophy and process. Record the raw score (1–10), calculate its weighted value, and provide concise notes summarizing reasoning quality and strategic logic.

**Category 2: Client Knowledge & Objectives (20%)** — Evaluate the team’s understanding of the client’s needs, mission, and liquidity requirements. Assign a score and a weighted percentage. Include short feedback highlighting alignment or gaps with MTWB’s objectives.

**Category 3: Portfolio Analysis (20%)** — Measure diversification, balance among sleeves, and risk-return calibration. Write a short note on strengths or weaknesses in the portfolio’s quantitative and qualitative justification.

**Category 4: Analytical & Financial Rigor (10%)** — Judge use of valuation techniques, financial reasoning, and data accuracy. Provide a score and brief comments on whether assumptions and metrics are well-grounded.

**Category 5: Communication & Presentation (5%)** — Evaluate clarity, formatting, tone, and professionalism. Assign a weighted score and record remarks on readability and report structure.

**Category 6: Creativity & Insight (10%)** — Score originality and conceptual innovation. Note how effectively creativity enhances understanding or strategy depth.

**Category 7: Critical Thinking & Trade-Off Discipline (10%)** — Evaluate awareness of alternatives, opportunity costs, and trade-offs. Offer feedback on whether reasoning is balanced and evidence-based.

Finally, compute the **Total Weighted Score (100%)** and make a clear **Recommendation** — *Advance to Semifinals, Borderline – Needs Revision, or Below Threshold*. Include one to two sentences summarizing rationale for the decision.

##### **Decision Protocol**

Weighted totals inform but do not dictate outcomes. Panel consensus prevails, and disqualifiers override numerical results. Replace statistical advancement probabilities with simple categorical outcomes:

**Advance to Semifinals / Borderline – Needs Revision / Below Threshold.**

##### **Fairness and Readability Standards**

- Reward clarity, logic, and originality regardless of linguistic polish.
- Recognize teams showing measurable learning and ethical reasoning.
- Maintain cross-judge consistency through anchors and calibration sessions.
- Prioritize substance and professionalism over style and embellishment.

##### **Final Evaluation Benchmark**

A top-tier Final Report should read like a **Wharton-endowment investment memo** — precise, coherent, numerically validated, ethically consistent, and aligned with MTWB’s philosophy of *sustainable community stewardship and disciplined growth*. Exceptional reports blend purpose, practicality, and professionalism in equal measure.


## Tab: RESOURCES > Sample Report

_Tab ID: `t.sjoreg73sayr`_

[https://docs.google.com/document/d/10WpsZdKfAgIoaabbIkuItuxxKkRJQJaQWIPBz_bYpnI/edit?usp=sharing](https://docs.google.com/document/d/10WpsZdKfAgIoaabbIkuItuxxKkRJQJaQWIPBz_bYpnI/edit?usp=sharing)

I understand you would like me to repeat the summary of the key learnings from the analysis of the "Gambler" team's report, but with all references to source numbers removed.

Here are the key learnings summarized in notes format:

##### **1. Strategic Asset Allocation & Risk Management**

- **Match Assets to Time Horizons:** Never align short-term liabilities with high-volatility assets. The team identified a short-term need for renovation cash but allocated it to volatile stocks like Lululemon and Amazon.
  - *Learning:* For goals under 5 years, prioritize capital preservation (Bonds, Money Market Funds) over aggressive growth.
- **Goal Segmentation is Critical:** Successfully identifying and separating client goals (e.g., Short-term Renovation vs. Long-term Company Building) is a strong foundation.
  - *Learning:* Explicitly partition the portfolio: "Portfolio A: Liquidity" vs. "Portfolio B: Growth."
- [image: Image of asset allocation pyramid]
- Getty Images

---

##### **2. Depth of Analysis vs. Description**

- **Thesis Over Description:** Avoid "Wikipedia-style" summaries. The report merely described what AMD does (manufacturing CPUs/GPUs) rather than analyzing *why* it is a superior investment at current valuations.
  - *Learning:* Your report must answer "Why this stock?" and "Why now?" using metrics (P/E, PEG, DCF) rather than product catalogs.
- **Sophisticated Screening:** Do not rely on simplistic filters. The team used basic "Top 10 stocks with highest EPS".
  - *Learning:* Use multi-factor screening (e.g., combining Valuation + Momentum + Quality factors) to demonstrate institutional-grade rigor.

---

##### **3. Client Personalization (The "Wow" Factor)**

- **Quantify the Qualitative:** The concept of the "Hilary Index" (50% ESG + 10% Consumer Satisfaction, etc.) was excellent in theory but lacked clear execution in the final selection.
  - *Learning:* Create a proprietary "Client Fit Score" for your team. Show the formula and apply it visibly to your top 3 holdings to prove you are listening to the client's values.

---

##### **4. Professionalism & Branding**

- **Brand Perception Matters:** Naming a wealth management team "Gambler" undermines credibility instantly.
  - *Learning:* Choose a name that evokes stability, stewardship, and foresight (e.g., *Praesidium*, *Sentinel*, *Vanguard*).
- **Quality Control is Non-Negotiable:** Errors like "Select the socks" (instead of stocks) and "moderately the riskness" signal a lack of care.
  - *Learning:* Assign a dedicated "Editor" role to proofread all charts and text before submission.
**Would you like to draft a specific "Client Fit" formula for your team based on these notes?**

To distinguish your team from competing portfolio managers, you must look past the surface-level persona of "Former NFL Player / Philanthropist" and analyze Connor Barwin as a **sophisticated, data-driven operator with a preference for tangible, "turnaround" assets.**

Most competitors will pitch a standard "Aggressive Growth" portfolio wrapped in generic ESG language. They will assume his MBA is just a credential and that his football background makes him risk-tolerant.

Here are the specific details others will ignore, and how to leverage them for a winning strategy:

##### **1. The "Distressed Asset" & Turnaround Psychology**

- **What Others See:** A Desire for "Long-term Growth."
- **What Others Will Ignore:** Barwin’s track record is defined by **taking neglected, undervalued assets and rehabilitating them through active management.**
  - *Evidence:* He renovated dilapidated parks (MTWB) and bought a relegated Italian basketball team (Trieste) to rebuild it. He doesn't buy "shiny" assets; he buys "broken" ones with high potential.
- 
- **Your Strategy Edge:** Instead of just pitching "Tech Growth Stocks" (which are expensive), pitch a **Value-Add Strategy.** Highlight investments in companies or sectors that are currently undervalued but have strong fundamentals for a turnaround. Frame the portfolio construction as "renovating" capital to build future value, mirroring his work with parks and sports teams.

##### **2. The Specificity of His "ESG" Definition (Place-Based Impact)**

- **What Others See:** A generic check-the-box request for "Socially Responsible Investing (ESG)."
- **What Others Will Ignore:** Barwin’s philanthropy is extremely specific: it is **hyper-local, tangible, and infrastructure-based.**
  - *Evidence:* He focuses on *public spaces*, *recreation centers*, and *urban revitalization*. He values the "S" (Social) regarding community cohesion and the "E" (Environment) regarding physical urban sustainability (solar panels, green spaces).
- 
- **Your Strategy Edge:** Avoid generic ESG ETFs that just hold Apple and Microsoft. Propose **Municipal Bonds (specifically Social Impact Bonds)**, **Green Infrastructure Funds**, or **Affordable Housing REITs.** Show him assets that build physical community infrastructure. This aligns with his belief that "strong communities are built through shared public spaces."

##### **3. The "Moneyball" Analytical Lens**

- **What Others See:** An ex-athlete who likely relies on "gut feeling" or needs concepts simplified.
- **What Others Will Ignore:** Barwin is a **Wharton MBA who specializes in strategy and data analytics.**
  - *Evidence:* He implemented player tracking data for the Eagles; he brought "Moneyball" analytics to his Italian basketball team. He explicitly stated Wharton taught him to "ask better questions" using data.
- 
- **Your Strategy Edge:** Do not dumb down the presentation. Use **attribution analysis** and **quantitative metrics** to justify your asset allocation. Show him the data behind the risk-reward ratios. Treat him like a Quantitative Analyst, not a donor. He wants to know the *mechanics* of how the portfolio wins, not just the projected result.

##### **4. The "Endowment Proof-of-Concept" (The $10k Withdrawal)**

- **What Others See:** A $10,000 annual withdrawal on a $500k account is mathematically negligible (2%). They will treat it as a non-issue.
- **What Others Will Ignore:** The *symbolism* of the withdrawal. Barwin is trying to prove that MTWB can function as a **perpetual endowment**.
  - *Evidence:* He wants to start distributions in Year 3. This is a test of the portfolio's yield capability. He wants to see the portfolio "work" to support the community immediately, even while growing.
- 
- **Your Strategy Edge:** Structure the portfolio to generate **natural yield** (dividends/interest) that covers the $10k without selling principal. Pitch this as "Self-Sustaining Stewardship." Show him that the portfolio creates its own cash flow for the community, proving the endowment model works early on.

##### **5. The Mathematical Aggression Required (The 12% Hurdle)**

- **What Others See:** A goal to turn $500k into $1.5M in 10 years.
- **What Others Will Ignore:** The math is incredibly difficult. To triple money in 10 years requires an **11.6% CAGR**. When you factor in the annual withdrawals starting in Year 3, the required return pushes toward **12.5% - 13%**.
- **Your Strategy Edge:** Be the only team honest enough to say, *"Standard public equities might not hit this target safely."* This opens the door to discuss **Alternative Investments** (Private Equity, Private Credit, or Venture Capital) which aligns with his history of high-risk/high-reward ventures (like the sports team). Acknowledge the difficulty of the goal to build trust, then provide the aggressive solution.

##### **6. "Horizontal Leadership" & Collaboration**

- **What Others See:** A client who wants to hand over the money and check back once a year.
- **What Others Will Ignore:** Barwin practices "Horizontal Leadership." He convenes stakeholders and wants to be part of the process.
  - *Evidence:* He designs parks *with* the neighbors, not *for* them.
- 
- **Your Strategy Edge:** Don't pitch a "Black Box" solution. Pitch a **Partnership Model.** Offer quarterly "Strategy Sessions" rather than just "Performance Reviews." Frame the relationship as you (the PM) being the "Assistant GM" to his "GM"—providing the tools and data for him to make the final strategic calls on the foundation's direction.

##### **Summary Pitch Hook**

*"Connor, most managers see a foundation portfolio. We see a revitalization project. Like your work with Vare Rec Center and Trieste, this capital requires a strategy that aggressively rehabilitates value, uses data to find inefficiencies, and generates yield to support the community immediately. To hit your $1.5M target, we can't just buy the market—we have to out-strategize it, just like you do."*

##### **1. The "Math Problem": You Will Miss the Target**

**The Issue:** You have allocated **50% to "Preserve"** (Utilities, Water, Low Volatility).  
**The Reality:** To turn $500k into $1.5M in 10 years requires an **11.6% Compounded Annual Growth Rate (CAGR)**. Once you factor in the $10k annual withdrawal, you actually need a return closer to **12.5% - 13%**.  
**The Fix:** Utilities and "Low Risk" stocks historically return 4-6%. If half your portfolio is making 5%, the other half needs to make 20%+ every single year to hit the goal. That is unrealistic.

- **Action:** Drastically reduce "Preserve" to **15-20%**. Increase "Pursue" and "Pioneer."
- **Justification to Client:** *"Connor, to hit your $1.5M target, we ran the math. A standard defensive portfolio falls short by $400k. We must structure this like a growth endowment, not a savings account."*

##### **2. Re-Branding "ESG": From Generic to "Place-Based"**

**The Issue:** You are using standard FactSet metrics (Board Diversity, GHG Emissions). Every team does this. It feels like "Corporate ESG."  
**The Reality:** Barwin doesn’t care about corporate boardrooms; he cares about **Playgrounds, Neighborhoods, and Solar Panels.  
The Fix:** Rename your "Z-Axis" or refine the criteria to **"Tangible Community Impact."**

- **Action:** Instead of just "Sustainalytics Scores," look for specific themes that match MTWB:
  - **Urban Infrastructure:** Companies that build the cities (e.g., Vulcan Materials, Xylem).
  - **Green Tech/Retrofit:** Companies that make buildings efficient (e.g., Johnson Controls, Carrier).
  - **Affordable Housing/REITs:** If allowed, these are perfect matches for his values.
- 
- **The Narrative:** Tell him you aren't just looking for "nice" companies; you are looking for companies that physically improve the world, just like MTWB does.

##### **3. Improving the "Point-Line-Plane" Metaphor**

**The Issue:** "Visual art theory" is a bit abstract. Barwin is a builder and a sports strategist.  
**The Fix:** Pivot the metaphor slightly to **"Architectural Design"** or **"Playbook Design."**

- **Action:** Keep the geometry, but explain it in his language.
  - *Point:* The Player (The Stock).
  - *Line:* The Strategy (The Data Trends).
  - *Plane:* The Field of Play (The Market Context).
- 
- **Better yet, link it to his park designs:**
  - *"Just as you assess a park using Grade, Drainage, and Accessibility, we assess stocks using Risk, Return, and Stewardship. The 'Stewardship Surface' is the blueprint."*
- 

##### **4. Optimize the "3P Sleeves" for a "Moneyball" Investor**

Barwin likes analytics ("Moneyball") and he likes yield (Endowment). Here is how to tweak your sleeves to sound smarter and more aggressive:

**Sleeve 1: Preserve (Change to "Foundation" or "Yield Core")**

- **Allocation:** Drop to ~20%.
- **Role:** Strictly to generate the $10k cash flow via dividends so you never have to sell principal.
- **Pitch:** *"This sleeve acts as the endowment's engine. It generates $15k in dividends annually, covering your $10k withdrawal with a safety buffer, so the rest of the portfolio can run fast."*
**Sleeve 2: Pursue (Change to "Compounders")**

- **Allocation:** Increase to ~50%.
- **Role:** The heavy lifting.
- **Pitch:** *"These are the franchise players. High ROIC, strong moats. We are looking for the 'All-Pros' of the industry."*
**Sleeve 3: Pioneer (Keep name, or use "Ventures")**

- **Allocation:** Increase to ~30%.
- **Role:** The aggressive growth needed to hit $1.5M.
- **Pitch:** *"You invested in a turnaround Italian basketball team because you saw value where others didn't. This sleeve does the same—investing in misunderstood or emerging sectors (AI infrastructure, Biotech) that provide the alpha to triple your capital."*

##### **5. The "Killer" Slide: The Stewardship Surface**

**Feedback on the visual:**  
The "Stewardship Surface" (Pareto Frontier) is your winning concept. **Do not lose this.**

- **How to sell it:** Most teams will say, "We picked high ESG stocks." You are saying, *"We mathematically calculated the trade-off between Profit and Purpose."*
- **Add a specific example:** Show a stock that is high return but low stewardship (e.g., a tobacco company) and show why it falls *below* the surface. Then show a stock like a Green Energy Utility that sits *on* the surface. This proves your model works.

##### **Summary Checklist for Semifinals:**

- **Aggression:** Acknowledge the difficulty of the $1.5M goal. Be honest that it requires risk.
- **Specificity:** Drop generic ESG language. Use "Urban Renewal" and "Community Infrastructure" language.
- **Data:** Emphasize that your 3D Matrix is quantitative, not qualitative. Barwin trusts numbers.
- **Yield:** Explain exactly how the $10k is paid (Dividends vs. Selling shares). Dividends are better.
**Team Name Idea (Optional):** Since "Greenox" sounds a bit generic, ensure your subtitle is strong.

- *Greenox: The Infrastructure of Impact.*
- *Greenox: Quantifying Community Value.*
